                                    <h2><a href="https://espndesmoines.com/news/030030-whats-next-in-the-investigation-of-the-learjet-crash-in-florida-that-killed-9/">What&#8217;s next in the investigation of the Learjet crash in Florida that killed 9?</a></h2>
                
                <div class="itemimg">
                    
                                            <a href="https://espndesmoines.com/news/030030-whats-next-in-the-investigation-of-the-learjet-crash-in-florida-that-killed-9/">

" data-large-file="https://news.sagacom.com/wp-content/blogs.dir/3/files/2026/11/AP26306601321530-scaled.jpg" />                        </a>
                    
                </div>
                <p>Investigators are just beginning the lengthy process of determining why a business jet plummeted from the sky in Florida after encountering a thunderstorm and slammed into the ground with such force that it created a crater and shattered the plane.</p>
            </div>

            
            <div class="item">
                <p class="momentdate incats"><span
                            class="time">2 days ago</span>
                    in <a href="/news/sections/national">National</a>, <a href="/news/sections/trending">Trending</a></p>

                                    <h2><a href="https://espndesmoines.com/news/030030-democrats-usher-in-a-new-era-with-their-resounding-sweep-of-the-house-and-the-senate/">Democrats usher in a new era with their resounding sweep of the House and the Senate</a></h2>

                <p>With their top two quarterbacks injured, the Baltimore Ravens were prepared to use any trick necessary in their attempt to keep the game competitive against Jacksonville. That included snapping the ball directly to Derrick Henry on quite a few occasions.</p>

                                    <h2><a href="https://espndesmoines.com/news/030030-cristiano-ronaldo-announces-his-international-retirement-from-portugal/">Cristiano Ronaldo announces his international retirement from Portugal</a></h2>
                
                <div class="itemimg">

		*/

		function sendEvent(action, label, scrollDistance, timing) {
		if ( 'undefined' === typeof MonsterInsightsObject || 'undefined' === typeof MonsterInsightsObject.sendEvent ) {
		return;
		}
			var paramName = action.toLowerCase();
	var fieldsArray = {
	send_to: 'G-P3NFKDZ3BH',
	non_interaction: true
	};
	fieldsArray[paramName] = label;

	if (arguments.length > 3) {
	fieldsArray.scroll_timing = timing
	MonsterInsightsObject.sendEvent('event', 'scroll_depth', fieldsArray);
	} else {
	MonsterInsightsObject.sendEvent('event', 'scroll_depth', fieldsArray);
	}
			}

		function calculateMarks(docHeight) {
		return {
		'25%' : parseInt(docHeight * 0.25, 10),
		'50%' : parseInt(docHeight * 0.50, 10),
		'75%' : parseInt(docHeight * 0.75, 10),
		/* Cushion to trigger 100% event in iOS */
		'100%': docHeight - 5
		};
		}

		function checkMarks(marks, scrollDistance, timing) {
		/* Check each active mark */
		$.each(marks, function(key, val) {
		if ( $.inArray(key, cache) === -1 && scrollDistance >= val ) {
		sendEvent('Percentage', key, scrollDistance, timing);
		cache.push(key);
		}
		});
		}

		function rounded(scrollDistance) {
		/* Returns String */
		return (Math.floor(scrollDistance/250) * 250).toString();
		}

		function init() {
		bindScrollDepth();
		}

		/*
		* Public Methods
		*/

		/* Reset Scroll Depth with the originally initialized options */
		$.scrollDepth.reset = function() {
		cache = [];
		lastPixelDepth = 0;
		$window.off('scroll.scrollDepth');
		bindScrollDepth();
		};

		/* Add DOM elements to be tracked */
		$.scrollDepth.addElements = function(elems) {

		if (typeof elems == "undefined" || !$.isArray(elems)) {
		return;
		}

		$.merge(options.elements, elems);

		/* If scroll event has been unbound from window, rebind */
		if (!scrollEventBound) {
		bindScrollDepth();
		}

		};

		/* Remove DOM elements currently tracked */
		$.scrollDepth.removeElements = function(elems) {

		if (typeof elems == "undefined" || !$.isArray(elems)) {
		return;
		}

		$.each(elems, function(index, elem) {

		var inElementsArray = $.inArray(elem, options.elements);
		var inCacheArray = $.inArray(elem, cache);

		if (inElementsArray != -1) {
		options.elements.splice(inElementsArray, 1);
		}

		if (inCacheArray != -1) {
		cache.splice(inCacheArray, 1);
		}

		});

		};

		/*
		* Throttle function borrowed from:
		* Underscore.js 1.5.2
		* https://underscorejs.org
		* (c) 2009-2013 Jeremy Ashkenas, DocumentCloud and Investigative Reporters & Editors
		* Underscore may be freely distributed under the MIT license.
		*/

		function throttle(func, wait) {
		var context, args, result;
		var timeout = null;
		var previous = 0;
		var later = function() {
		previous = new Date;
		timeout = null;
		result = func.apply(context, args);
		};
		return function() {
		var now = new Date;
		if (!previous) previous = now;
		var remaining = wait - (now - previous);
		context = this;
		args = arguments;
		if (remaining <= 0) {
		clearTimeout(timeout);
		timeout = null;
		previous = now;
		result = func.apply(context, args);
		} else if (!timeout) {
		timeout = setTimeout(later, remaining);
		}
		return result;
		};
		}

		/*
		* Scroll Event
		*/

		function bindScrollDepth() {

		scrollEventBound = true;

		$window.on('scroll.scrollDepth', throttle(function() {
		/*
		* We calculate document and window height on each scroll event to
		* account for dynamic DOM changes.
		*/

		var docHeight = $(document).height(),
		winHeight = window.innerHeight ? window.innerHeight : $window.height(),
		scrollDistance = $window.scrollTop() + winHeight,

		/* Recalculate percentage marks */
		marks = calculateMarks(docHeight),

		/* Timing */
		timing = +new Date - startTime;

		checkMarks(marks, scrollDistance, timing);
		}, 500));

		}

		init();
		};

		/* UMD export */
		return $.scrollDepth;

		}));

		jQuery.scrollDepth();
		} else {
		setTimeout(monsterinsights_scroll_tracking_load, 200);
		}
		}
		monsterinsights_scroll_tracking_load();
		});
		}
		/* End MonsterInsights Scroll Tracking */
			/* MonsterInsights Conversion Event */
	jQuery(document).ready(function() {
		jQuery('a[data-mi-conversion-event]')
			.off('click.monsterinsightsConversion')
			.on('click.monsterinsightsConversion', function() {
				if ( typeof(__gtagTracker) !== 'undefined' && __gtagTracker ) {
					var $link = jQuery(this);
					var eventName = $link.attr('data-mi-event-name');
					if ( typeof eventName === 'undefined' || ! eventName ) {
						// Fallback to first word of the <a> tag, lowercase, strip html
						var text = $link.text().trim();
						text = text.replace(/(<([^>]+)>)/gi, '').toLowerCase();
						var firstWord = text.split(/\s+/)[0] || '';

						if ( firstWord ) {
							eventName = 'click-' + firstWord;
						} else {
							eventName = $link.parent().hasClass('wp-block-image') ? 'image-click' : 'button-click';
						}
					}
					__gtagTracker('event', 'mi-' + eventName);
				}
			});
	});
	/* End MonsterInsights Conversion Event */

<p id="mwDg"><b id="mwDw">WDEE-FM</b> (97.3 FM, "Sunny 97.3") is a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio_station" title="Radio station" class="mw-redirect" id="mwEA">radio station</a> licensed to <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reed_City,_Michigan" title="Reed City, Michigan" id="mwEQ">Reed City, Michigan</a>, with studios in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Rapids,_Michigan" title="Big Rapids, Michigan" id="mwEg">Big Rapids</a>. The station plays the Classic Hits from 1955 through 2006.</p>

<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" id="mwEw" data-mw='{"autoGenerated":true}'/></section><section data-mw-section-id="1" id="mwFA" aria-labelledby="History"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="History">History</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=WDEE-FM&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: History"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<section data-mw-section-id="2" id="mwFQ" aria-labelledby="Beginnings_as_WDEE"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Beginnings_as_WDEE">Beginnings as WDEE</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=WDEE-FM&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Beginnings as WDEE"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>

<p id="mwFg">WDEE originally began as an AM station broadcasting on 1500<span typeof="mw:Entity" id="mwFw"> </span>kHz, first going on the air in 1981, and under the ownership of David A. Carmine, a well-known country music radio personality from Detroit known by his on-air name of Dave Carr.  Carmine had moved to <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harrison,_Michigan" title="Harrison, Michigan" id="mwGA">Harrison, Michigan</a>, in 1973 to put his first station on the air in 1975, known then as <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WKKM-LP" title="WKKM-LP" id="mwGQ">WKKM</a>.  WDEE first broadcast with its own independent staff and programming originating from downtown Reed City at 410 W. Upton Avenue.  By the end of the decade, WDEE was reduced to a simulcast outlet of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WTWS" title="WTWS" id="mwGg">WKKM</a> in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harrison,_Michigan" title="Harrison, Michigan" id="mwGw">Harrison</a> before finally being silenced in October 1992.</p>

<p id="mwHA">Prior to this version of WDEE, the WDEE call sign was used on a well known 50,000<span typeof="mw:Entity" id="mwHQ">–</span>watt AM station in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit" title="Detroit" id="mwHg">Detroit</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michigan" title="Michigan" id="mwHw">Michigan</a>, on the same frequency of 1500<span typeof="mw:Entity" id="mwIA"> </span>kHz (now known as <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WLQV" title="WLQV" id="mwIQ">WLQV</a>).  WDEE in Detroit was one of the first radio stations to program a country music format using top 40 methods and was very popular.  Like the original WDEE, Reed City's WDEE was a country station.</p>

<p id="mwIg">From 1960 to 1967 the call letters WDEE were assigned to a daytime AM station in Hamden, Connecticut broadcasting on frequency 1220.</p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="3" id="mwIw" aria-labelledby="Sale_to_current_owner"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Sale_to_current_owner">Sale to current owner</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=WDEE-FM&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Sale to current owner"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>

<p id="mwJA">In January 1994, WDEE was sold to Steven V. Beilfuss known to northwestern Michigan listeners as Steve Masters and best known for his tenure at 100,000 watt stations WLDR-FM in Traverse City and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WJZQ" title="WJZQ" id="mwJQ">WKJF</a>-FM in Cadillac. Beilfuss put the station back on the air in 1994 with an <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oldies" title="Oldies" id="mwJg">Oldies</a> format.  Studios and offices were established at 207<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1154941027" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles mw:Transclusion" about="#mwt13" id="mwJw" data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Fraction/styles.css"},"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"frac","href":"./Template:Frac"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"1"},"2":{"wt":"2"}},"i":0}}]}'>.mw-parser-output .frac{white-space:nowrap}.mw-parser-output .frac .num,.mw-parser-output .frac .den{font-size:80%;line-height:0;vertical-align:super}.mw-parser-output .frac .den{vertical-align:sub}.mw-parser-output .sr-only{border:0;clip:rect(0,0,0,0);clip-path:polygon(0px 0px,0px 0px,0px 0px);height:1px;margin:-1px;overflow:hidden;padding:0;position:absolute;width:1px}</style><span class="frac" about="#mwt13" id="mwKA"><span class="num">1</span><span typeof="mw:Entity">⁄</span><span class="den">2</span></span> W. Upton Avenue in Reed City, not far from the original location.</p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="4" id="mwKQ" aria-labelledby="Birth_of_WDEE-FM"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Birth_of_WDEE-FM">Birth of WDEE-FM</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=WDEE-FM&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Birth of WDEE-FM"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>

<p id="mwKg">Originally a weak daytime-only AM signal of 250 watts, ownership then successfully applied for an FM license for 97.3<span typeof="mw:Entity" id="mwKw"> </span>MHz, which made its debut as WDEE-FM as a full simulcast of its AM sister-station. At that time, the station was known as "Classic Hits 97.3." At around this same time, studios and offices were relocated to 101 S. Higbee Street in Reed City.</p>

<p id="mwLA">Eventually, programming was separated between the two stations, with a classic country format on WDEE. After ownership chose not to renew WDEE's license,<sup about="#mwt15" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-2" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-2"}}'><a href="#cite_note-2" id="mwLQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwLg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwLw">[</span>2<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwMA">]</span></span></a></sup> WDEE-FM was left as the only license owned by Beilfuss Broadcasting and continues to broadcast today.</p>

<p id="mwMQ">Shortly after the turn of the 21st Century, WDEE-FM re-located its studios from Reed City into downtown <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Rapids,_Michigan" title="Big Rapids, Michigan" id="mwMg">Big Rapids</a>. Their studios are currently located at 120 North Michigan Ave, Suite 4 in the plaza along with Family Dollar and the Michigan Secretary of State's office.</p>

href="https://publicfiles.fcc.gov/am-profile/KRNT" class="external text">Public file</a></li><li><a rel="mw:ExtLink nofollow" href="https://enterpriseefiling.fcc.gov/dataentry/public/tv/publicFacilityDetails.html?facilityId=58534" class="external text">LMS</a></li></ul></div></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Webcast" title="Webcast">Webcast</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a rel="mw:ExtLink nofollow" href="https://espndesmoines.com/live-to-live-sports" class="external text">Listen live</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Website</th><td class="infobox-data"><span class="url"><a rel="mw:ExtLink nofollow" href="https://espndesmoines.com" class="external text">espndesmoines<wbr/>.com</a></span></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p id="mwEQ"><b id="mwEg">KRNT</b> (1350 <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AM_broadcasting" title="AM broadcasting" id="mwEw">AM</a>) is a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio_station" title="Radio station" class="mw-redirect" id="mwFA">radio station</a> in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Des_Moines,_Iowa" title="Des Moines, Iowa" id="mwFQ">Des Moines, Iowa</a>, United States, broadcasting a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sports_radio" title="Sports radio" id="mwFg">sports</a> format. The station is owned by <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saga_Communications" title="Saga Communications" id="mwFw">Saga Communications</a> through licensee Saga Communications of Iowa, LLC; it operates as part of Saga's Des Moines Radio Group.</p>

<p id="mwGA">Studios are located on Locust Street in Des Moines, with its 4-tower transmitter array located on King Avenue in Des Moines.</p>

<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" id="mwGQ" data-mw='{"autoGenerated":true}'/></section><section data-mw-section-id="1" id="mwGg" aria-labelledby="History"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="History">History</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=KRNT&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: History"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<figure class="mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mwGw"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:1926_Agricultural_Seed_Company_advertisement_extract_referencing_Clarinda,_Iowa_radio_station_KSO.gif" class="mw-file-description" id="mwHA"><img resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:1926_Agricultural_Seed_Company_advertisement_extract_referencing_Clarinda,_Iowa_radio_station_KSO.gif" src="//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c4/1926_Agricultural_Seed_Company_advertisement_extract_referencing_Clarinda%2C_Iowa_radio_station_KSO.gif/250px-1926_Agricultural_Seed_Company_advertisement_extract_referencing_Clarinda%2C_Iowa_radio_station_KSO.gif?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail" decoding="async" loading="lazy" srcset="//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c4/1926_Agricultural_Seed_Company_advertisement_extract_referencing_Clarinda%2C_Iowa_radio_station_KSO.gif/500px-1926_Agricultural_Seed_Company_advertisement_extract_referencing_Clarinda%2C_Iowa_radio_station_KSO.gif?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail 2x" data-file-width="571" data-file-height="488" data-file-type="bitmap" height="214" width="250" class="mw-file-element" id="mwHQ"/></a><figcaption id="mwHg">A 1926 advertisement extract noted KSO's "Keep Serving Others" slogan.<sup about="#mwt23" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-1926ad_2-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"1926ad"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-1926ad-2"}}'><a href="#cite_note-1926ad-2" id="mwHw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwIA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwIQ">[</span>2<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwIg">]</span></span></a></sup></figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mwIw"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:1933_Des_Moines_Register_and_Tribune_advertisement_referencing_Des_Moines,_Iowa_radio_station_KSO.gif" class="mw-file-description" id="mwJA"><img resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:1933_Des_Moines_Register_and_Tribune_advertisement_referencing_Des_Moines,_Iowa_radio_station_KSO.gif" src="//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b9/1933_Des_Moines_Register_and_Tribune_advertisement_referencing_Des_Moines%2C_Iowa_radio_station_KSO.gif/250px-1933_Des_Moines_Register_and_Tribune_advertisement_referencing_Des_Moines%2C_Iowa_radio_station_KSO.gif?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail" decoding="async" loading="lazy" srcset="//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b9/1933_Des_Moines_Register_and_Tribune_advertisement_referencing_Des_Moines%2C_Iowa_radio_station_KSO.gif/500px-1933_Des_Moines_Register_and_Tribune_advertisement_referencing_Des_Moines%2C_Iowa_radio_station_KSO.gif?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail 2x" data-file-width="518" data-file-height="608" data-file-type="bitmap" height="293" width="250" class="mw-file-element" id="mwJQ"/></a><figcaption id="mwJg">In 1932, KSO was moved to Des Moines, becoming KRNT in 1935.<sup about="#mwt24" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-3" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-3"}}'><a href="#cite_note-3" id="mwJw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwKA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwKQ">[</span>3<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwKg">]</span></span></a></sup></figcaption></figure>

<section data-mw-section-id="2" id="mwKw" aria-labelledby="KSO"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="KSO">KSO</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=KRNT&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: KSO"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwLA">The station was first licensed on October 9, 1925, to the A. A. Berry Seed Company in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clarinda,_Iowa" title="Clarinda, Iowa" id="mwLQ">Clarinda, Iowa</a>, transmitting on 1240 kHz, with the call sign KSO.<sup about="#mwt25" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-4" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-4"}}'><a href="#cite_note-4" id="mwLg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwLw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwMA">[</span>4<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwMQ">]</span></span></a></sup> The call letters reflected the slogan "Keep Serving Others".<sup about="#mwt26" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-1926ad_2-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"1926ad"}}'><a href="#cite_note-1926ad-2" id="mwMg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwMw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwNA">[</span>2<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwNQ">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwNg">In 1926, an adverse legal decision briefly eliminated the ability of the U.S. government to assign station frequencies and powers, and as of December 31, 1926, KSO was reported to now be at 740 kHz.<sup about="#mwt27" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-5" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-5"}}'><a href="#cite_note-5" id="mwNw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwOA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwOQ">[</span>5<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwOg">]</span></span></a></sup> The next year government control was re-established with the formation of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Radio_Commission" title="Federal Radio Commission" id="mwOw">Federal Radio Commission</a> (FRC), and on July 1, 1927, KSO was reassigned to 1320 kHz.<sup about="#mwt28" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-6" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-6"}}'><a href="#cite_note-6" id="mwPA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwPQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwPg">[</span>6<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwPw">]</span></span></a></sup> Effective November 11, 1928, with the implementation of the FRC's <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Order_40" title="General Order 40" id="mwQA">General Order 40</a>, the station moved to 1380 kHz.<sup about="#mwt29" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-7" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-7"}}'><a href="#cite_note-7" id="mwQQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwQg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwQw">[</span>7<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwRA">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwRQ">On June 26, 1931, the Cowles family, publishers of the <i id="mwRg"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Des_Moines_Register" title="Des Moines Register" class="mw-redirect" id="mwRw">Des Moines Register</a></i> and <i id="mwSA"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Des_Moines_Tribune" title="Des Moines Tribune" id="mwSQ">Tribune</a></i>, bought KSO,<sup about="#mwt32" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-history_8-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"history"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-history-8"}}'><a href="#cite_note-history-8" id="mwSg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwSw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwTA">[</span>8<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwTQ">]</span></span></a></sup> via the Iowa Broadcasting Company. The new owners received permission to move KSO from Clarinda to Des Moines, and to begin broadcasting on 1370 kHz. KSO made its debut broadcast from the new location on November 5, 1932.<sup about="#mwt33" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-9" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-9"}}'><a href="#cite_note-9" id="mwTg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwTw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwUA">[</span>9<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwUQ">]</span></span></a></sup> In 1934, KSO was reassigned to 1320 kHz.</p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="3" id="mwUg" aria-labelledby="KRNT"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="KRNT">KRNT</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=KRNT&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: KRNT"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwUw">On March 17, 1935, the callsign KSO was transferred to the former <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KXNO_(AM)" title="KXNO (AM)" id="mwVA">KWCR</a> on 1430 kHz, which was relocated from Cedar Rapids to Des Moines. Concurrently, the call letters of the original KSO on 1320 kHz were changed to KRNT.<sup about="#mwt34" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-bc040135_10-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"bc040135"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-bc040135-10"}}'><a href="#cite_note-bc040135-10" id="mwVQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwVg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwVw">[</span>10<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwWA">]</span></span></a></sup> At that time, both stations were owned and operated by subsidiary corporations of the <i id="mwWQ">Des Moines Register-Tribune</i>.<sup about="#mwt35" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-bc040135_10-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"bc040135"}}'><a href="#cite_note-bc040135-10" id="mwWg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwWw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwXA">[</span>10<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwXQ">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwXg">In March 1941, stations on 1320 kHz, including KRNT, were moved to the 1350 kHz, as part of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_American_Regional_Broadcasting_Agreement" title="North American Regional Broadcasting Agreement" id="mwXw">North American Regional Broadcasting Agreement</a>.<sup about="#mwt36" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-11" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-11"}}'><a href="#cite_note-11" id="mwYA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwYQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwYg">[</span>11<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwYw">]</span></span></a></sup> In August 1941, the Federal Communications Commission (FCC) began implementation of a "duopoly" rule, which restricted licensees from operating more than one radio station in a given market.<sup about="#mwt37" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-12" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-12"}}'><a href="#cite_note-12" id="mwZA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwZQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwZg">[</span>12<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwZw">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwaA">KRNT won a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peabody_Award" title="Peabody Award" class="mw-redirect" id="mwaQ">Peabody Award</a> in 1945 for outstanding reporting of news.<sup about="#mwt40" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-13" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-13"}}'><a href="#cite_note-13" id="mwag"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwaw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwbA">[</span>13<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwbQ">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwbg">Then-owners Cowles Media started up a sister station, KRNT-FM (104.5) in 1948, but took it off the air and returned the license in 1955. In 1970, they signed on a second <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KSTZ" title="KSTZ" id="mwbw">KRNT-FM</a> (102.5 FM).<sup about="#mwt43" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-14" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-14"}}'><a href="#cite_note-14" id="mwcA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwcQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwcg">[</span>14<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwcw">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwdA">For years, KRNT carried an <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adult_standards" title="Adult standards" id="mwdQ">adult standards</a> format, supplemented by sports programming (most notably the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Cubs" title="Chicago Cubs" id="mwdg">Chicago Cubs</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drake_Bulldogs" title="Drake Bulldogs" id="mwdw">Drake University athletics</a>). On September 10, 2015, the adult standards format was moved to sister station <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KAZR" title="KAZR" id="mweA">KAZR</a>'s HD2 subchannel and translator K283CC 104.5 FM. KRNT was rebranded <i id="mweQ">ESPN Des Moines</i> as it became the market's <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ESPN_Radio" title="ESPN Radio" id="mweg">ESPN Radio</a> affiliate.<sup about="#mwt44" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-15" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-15"}}'><a href="#cite_note-15" id="mwew"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwfA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwfQ">[</span>15<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwfg">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt47" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-16" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-16"}}'><a href="#cite_note-16" id="mwfw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwgA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwgQ">[</span>16<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwgg">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwgw">In April 2022, station owner Saga Communications requested a waiver to change the call sign back to the original KSO. However, broadcasting stations were now generally required to have four-letter call signs, and the FCC was unwilling to make an exception.<sup about="#mwt48" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-17" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-17"}}'><a href="#cite_note-17" id="mwhA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwhQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwhg">[</span>17<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwhw">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section></section><section data-mw-section-id="4" id="mwiA" aria-labelledby="Programming"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Programming">Programming</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=KRNT&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Programming"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwiQ">KRNT features programming from <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ESPN_Radio" title="ESPN Radio" id="mwig">ESPN Radio</a>.<sup about="#mwt51" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-18" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-18"}}'><a href="#cite_note-18" id="mwiw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwjA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwjQ">[</span>18<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwjg">]</span></span></a></sup>  In addition, it serves as the broadcast outlet for several sports teams:<sup about="#mwt54" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-19" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-19"}}'><a href="#cite_note-19" id="mwjw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwkA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwkQ">[</span>19<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwkg">]</span></span></a></sup></p>
<ul id="mwkw"><li id="mwlA"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drake_Bulldogs" title="Drake Bulldogs" id="mwlQ">Drake University</a> athletics (<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drake_Bulldogs_football" title="Drake Bulldogs football" id="mwlg">football</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drake_Bulldogs_men's_basketball" title="Drake Bulldogs men's basketball" id="mwlw">men's</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drake_Bulldogs_women's_basketball" title="Drake Bulldogs women's basketball" id="mwmA">women's</a> basketball)</li>
<li id="mwmQ"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Cubs" title="Chicago Cubs" id="mwmg">Chicago Cubs</a> baseball</li></ul>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="5" id="mwmw" aria-labelledby="References"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="References">References</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=KRNT&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: References"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
